      "response": "## Decision — **cannot yet be decided; place C-2026-04105 on hold**\n\nI cannot establish coverage or calculate a defensible payment because the claim’s substantive record was not available in the connected organisation records. Exact searches for **C-2026-04105** found no matching enterprise file or Teams discussion, and the Warranty Operations claim-evidence folder contains twelve other claim reports but none for C-2026-04105.\n\nThis is **not a decline** and should not be settled at zero. It is an evidence/data hold.\n\n### What is established\n\n- **Claimant:** Fabrikam Service Partners.\n- **Applicable partner agreement:** **SPA-2023-FAB-IN**, because it appoints Fabrikam as the authorised partner for India.\n- **Settlement currency:** **INR**.\n- **Partner-specific payment terms:** labour is reimbursed using the operation’s flat-rate allowance and the regional hourly rate in force on the repair date; parts are reimbursed at the India list price for the part actually fitted; **no handling uplift** is payable.\n\n### What is not established, so coverage cannot be selected\n\nThe available sources do not identify for this claim:\n\n1. asset serial number and product family;\n2. installation region/site;\n3. commissioning date;\n4. repair date;\n5. latest running-hours reading at or before repair;\n6. failed component and cause;\n7. operation code and claimed labour;\n8. part number actually fitted and quantity; or\n9. whether an inspection report exists where an exclusion depends on its findings.\n\nThose fields are necessary to select the governing warranty instrument and determine where the repair falls within the applicable time/hour limit. Under **POL-WAR-4.2**, the default is 24 months from commissioning or 6,000 hours, whichever occurs first; a serial-specific current TSB takes priority, followed by the regional addendum. The policy also says that if the commissioning record is absent, coverage cannot be determined and the claim must be held while the installing partner supplies the commissioning certificate.\n\nIf this is an India-installed asset, **ADD-IN-2.1** normally varies the period to **18 months or 5,000 hours**, whichever occurs first, unless a higher-precedence TSB names the asset’s serial range and covers the failed component.\n\n### Amount payable now\n\n| Element | Amount | Reason |\n|---|---:|---|\n| Labour | **Not determinable** | Operation code, repair date and applicable flat-rate hours are unavailable. |\n| Parts | **Not determinable** | Part actually fitted, quantity and repair-date price basis are unavailable. |\n| Partner handling/uplift | **INR 0** | Fabrikam’s agreement expressly provides no handling uplift. |\n| **Total** | **Not determinable** | Coverage and the labour/parts inputs have not been established. |\n\n### Next action\n\n1. **Warranty Operations/adjudicator:** put C-2026-04105 on **Evidence/Data Hold — not declined**.\n2. **Claim-system owner or adjudicator:** restore or provide the claim-system record containing the nine fields listed above, including the asset-registry commissioning date and the latest hours reading at or before repair.\n3. **Fabrikam Service Partners:** supply the commissioning certificate if the registry has no commissioning date, and provide the inspection report if the claimed failure could engage an exclusion.\n4. Once those facts are available, apply the current serial/component-specific TSB first, then the regional addendum, then POL-WAR-4.2; calculate labour from the repair-date rate card and flat-rate allowance, and price the part actually fitted in INR with no uplift.\n\n### Draft recording status\n\nI **could not record the hold/evidence request as a draft in the service claim system** because no service-claim-system connector or accessible claim list was available in this run. I did not create a substitute record elsewhere, because that would not satisfy the requirement that authority and claim actions be recorded against the claim itself.\n\n### Source conflict noted\n\nThe FY26 Q2 review deck contains a stale statement that 24 months/6,000 hours applies across all regions. That conflicts with the controlled India addendum. For an India installation, the controlled addendum’s 18-month/5,000-hour position applies unless displaced by a current, applicable serial-specific TSB; the review deck should not be used to adjudicate this claim.\n\n## Sources\n\n1. `claim-search` Referenced tool result\n2. `evidence-folder` Referenced tool result\n3. `teams-search` Referenced tool result\n4. `fabrikam-agreement` [SPA-2023-FAB-IN — Fabrikam Service Partners](<https://microsoftapc.sharepoint.com/teams/ContosoFieldService/Warranty Operations/04-PartnerAgreements/SPA-2023-FAB-IN Fabrikam Service Partners.docx>)\n5. `global-policy` [POL-WAR-4.2 — Contoso Industrial Global Warranty Policy v4.2](<https://microsoftapc.sharepoint.com/teams/ContosoFieldService/Warranty Operations/01-Policy/POL-WAR-4.2 Contoso Industrial Global Warranty Policy v4.2.docx>)\n6. `india-addendum` [ADD-IN-2.1 — Regional Addendum, India](<https://microsoftapc.sharepoint.com/teams/ContosoFieldService/Warranty Operations/01-Policy/ADD-IN-2.1 Regional Addendum - India v2.1.docx>)\n7. `q2-review` [FY26 Q2 Warranty Review](<https://microsoftapc.sharepoint.com/teams/ContosoFieldService/Warranty Operations/05-Reviews/FY26-Q2-Warranty-Review.pptx>)",

Amount: not established.**\n\nI could not complete the requested review because the service-claim-system record for **C-2026-04108** was not available through the connected tools. That record is needed to establish the asset/serial number, installation region, commissioning date, applicable running-hours reading, repair date and operation, fitted parts, quantities, service partner, and submitted amounts. Contoso’s own guidance says claims are adjudicated from the claim-system record and that the relevant hours are the latest reading held at or before the repair date.\n\nI also found **no C-2026-04108 evidence file** among the 12 records in the Warranty Operations claim-evidence folder; the available files cover other claims such as C-2026-04101, C-2026-04109, C-2026-04114 and later references. Exact Teams searches for the claim reference returned no messages.\n\n### Instrument position\nThe governing instrument cannot be selected without the claim’s asset serial, installation region and failed component. Under **POL-WAR-4.2 clause 1.4**, precedence is: (1) a current technical service bulletin naming the asset’s serial range, (2) the regional addendum, then (3) the global policy. The same policy gives the unvaried baseline as **24 months from commissioning or 6,000 running hours, whichever occurs first**.\n\nFor example, **TSB-C-0051** would override the regional addendum only if the claim concerns the hydraulic circuit or compressor of a 4000-series chiller in serial range **CIE-4000-CH-01200 to CIE-4000-CH-01850**; it extends coverage to **36 months or 8,000 hours, whichever occurs first**. There is currently no claim data allowing me to determine whether that or any other bulletin applies.\n\n### Amount\nNo defensible labour, parts or partner-specific amount can be calculated without the claim’s operation code, fitted part number and quantity, repair date/region, and service partner. I have therefore not substituted assumptions or used a neighbouring claim.\n\n### Required next action\nA Warranty Operations adjudicator or system administrator should retrieve or restore access to the **C-2026-04108** claim-system record and provide the asset, repair, hours, partner, parts and claimed-cost fields. An exact search of the Warranty Operations claim-evidence folder found reports for other claims but no report for **C-2026-04112**; exact searches of Teams messages also returned no match.\n\n### What is missing\nBefore an adjudicator can approve or decline this claim, we need the service claim system record showing:\n\n- asset serial number and product family;\n- installation region and service partner;\n- commissioning date;\n- repair date and submission date;\n- latest running-hours reading held at or before the repair date;\n- failed component, failure findings and operation code;\n- part number and quantity actually fitted;\n- labour hours claimed;\n- prior service/repair history; and\n- any inspection report or other exclusion-related evidence.\n\nWithout those facts I cannot determine which instrument governs, calculate the applicable time/hour limit, test exclusions, select the correct labour and parts rate cards, apply partner-specific terms, or state a defensible amount payable.\n\n### Governing framework once the claim record is available\nThe starting instrument is **POL-WAR-4.2**, but it can be displaced by (1) a current Technical Service Bulletin naming the asset’s serial range, then (2) the regional addendum. The policy baseline is **24 months from commissioning or 6,000 running hours, whichever occurs first**. It also says that where the asset registry has no commissioning record, coverage cannot be determined: the claim must be held and the commissioning certificate requested; installation or despatch dates must not be substituted.\n\nThe precise governing instrument cannot be selected until the serial number, region and failed component are known. For example, current records include materially different rules for India installations and for specified bulletin serial ranges, so applying the global baseline without the claim data would be unsafe.\n\n### Amount payable\n**Not calculable at present.**\n\n| Element | Current position |\n|---|---:|\n| Labour | Cannot calculate — operation code, claimed hours, repair date/rate card and partner are unknown |\n| Parts | Cannot calculate — actual fitted part, quantity, territory list price and repair date are unknown |\n| Partner terms | Cannot calculate — partner/agreement and settlement currency are unknown |\n| **Total** | **Undetermined** |\n\n### Required next action\n1. **Claims-system owner/adjudicator:** open C-2026-04112 and provide or restore access to the complete claim and asset record listed above.\n2. **Installing/service partner:** if the asset registry lacks the commissioning date, supply the commissioning certificate.\n3. **Warranty Operations:** once those records are available, determine bulletin/addendum applicability, coverage, exclusions and the reimbursable amount.\n\n### Claim-system draft\nI was **not able to record a draft hold/evidence request** because no service claim system connector or claim record was available in this task environment. The records needed to establish the repair, commissioning date, asset location, running hours, partner, operation code, claimed hours, and part actually fitted are therefore missing from the evidence available to me.\n\n### Warranty position\n\n- **TSB-C-0051 does not extend this asset’s coverage:** it applies only to serials **CIE-4000-CH-01200 through CIE-4000-CH-01850**; serial **01904** is outside that range. The bulletin is also limited to hydraulic-circuit and compressor repairs.\n- Subject to any other applicable bulletin or repair warranty, the governing regional addendum depends on where the asset is installed. If this is an India installation, **ADD-IN-2.1** varies standard coverage to **18 months from commissioning or 5,000 running hours, whichever occurs first**. The global policy says that where the commissioning record is absent, coverage cannot be determined and the claim must be held for the commissioning certificate.\n- I could not establish the commissioning date, latest valid running-hours reading at or before repair, repair date, or whether a prior warranty replacement created a 90-day repair warranty. Accordingly, I cannot place this repair inside or outside the applicable coverage period.\n\n### Payable figure\n\n**Current adjudicable amount: not established — do not treat this as INR 0.** A zero settlement would imply a supported decline, which the available records do not justify.\n\nFor orientation only, if the missing claim record were to show all of the following—India/Fabrikam, a covered control-board replacement after 1 April 2026, operation **CTRL-BD-RR**, at least 1.5 hours claimed, and one **P-44900** actually fitted—the rate-card calculation would be:\n\n- Labour: **1.5 h × INR 1,450 = INR 2,175**\n- Parts: **P-44900 = INR 67,400**\n- Partner uplift: **INR 0** (Fabrikam’s agreement grants no handling uplift)\n- Conditional total: **INR 69,575**\n\nThat is **not** the adjudicated payable figure for C-2026-04113; it is only a conditional illustration because the actual operation, part, partner, repair date and coverage facts were not retrievable. The serial is **potentially within extended, component-specific warranty**, but the essential claim-system facts—commissioning date, qualifying running-hours reading, installation region, and claimed component—were not available.\n\n### What can be established\n\n- **CIE-4000-CH-01701 is inside the serial range CIE-4000-CH-01200 to CIE-4000-CH-01850 covered by current TSB-C-0051.** That bulletin extends coverage **only for the hydraulic circuit and compressor** to **36 months from commissioning or 8,000 running hours, whichever occurs first**. It is effective from 15 January 2026.\n- TSB-C-0051 is the governing instrument **if C-2026-04119 concerns the hydraulic circuit or compressor**, because POL-WAR-4.2 clause 1.4 gives precedence to a current bulletin that names the asset’s serial range. The bulletin document also overrides any conflicting service-system applicability index.\n- If the claim concerns another component, TSB-C-0051 does not extend that component’s warranty. The applicable regional addendum and then the global policy would govern. The global baseline is **24 months/6,000 hours**; for an India installation, ADD-IN-2.1 varies this to **18 months/5,000 hours**.\n\n### Missing facts preventing a yes/no answer\n\nI could not retrieve a claim-system or evidence record for **C-2026-04119 / CIE-4000-CH-01701**. Searches of the Warranty Operations library and Teams returned no exact matching claim evidence or discussion.\n\nThe claim-system record therefore still needs to establish:\n\n1. **Date of commissioning** — required to test the 36-month or standard regional calendar limit. Under POL-WAR-4.2 clause 2.3, if the commissioning record is absent, coverage cannot be determined; the claim must be held and the commissioning certificate requested. Dispatch or installation date cannot be substituted.\n2. **Latest running-hours reading at or before 18 June 2026** — for TSB-C-0051 it must not have passed **8,000 hours**; the applicable claim-system reading, not an unsupported estimate, must be used.\n3. **Claimed operation/component** — it must be hydraulic-circuit or compressor work for the 36-month/8,000-hour extension to apply.\n4. **Installation region** — needed if the claimed repair is outside the TSB’s component scope.\n\n### Conditional warranty position at 18 June 2026\n\n- **Hydraulic-circuit or compressor repair:** in warranty only if the asset had not reached the earlier of **36 months from commissioning** and **8,000 running hours** by the repair date.\n- **Any other component:** assess under the applicable regional period and hours limit; there is not enough information to do that here.\n\n### Next action\n\n- **Adjudicator:** place **C-2026-04119 on hold / evidence required** rather than approve or decline it.\n- **Claim owner or service partner:** confirm the commissioning certificate, installation region and claimed operation/component.\n- **Warranty Operations:** verify the latest running-hours reading held in the claim system at or before **2026-06-18**.\n- If those facts show a hydraulic/compressor repair within both TSB limits, adjudicate under **funding code TSB, reference TSB-C-0051**.\n\nI could not record the hold/evidence request as a draft because no service-claim-system write interface was available in this task. Under **POL-WAR-4.2**, a serial-specific technical bulletin takes precedence over the regional addendum, which takes precedence over the global policy. The baseline is 24 months from commissioning or 6,000 running hours, whichever occurs first, unless varied; if the commissioning record is absent, the claim must be held and the commissioning certificate requested rather than substituting another date.\n\nThey are also necessary to calculate payment: labour is limited to the lesser of the current flat-rate allowance and hours actually claimed; parts and any handling uplift depend on the territory rate card, part actually fitted, and partner agreement.\n\n### Required next action\n**Warranty Operations / the adjudicator** should open C-2026-04120 in the service claim system and obtain the fields listed above, plus any inspection report needed to assess an exclusion.
